Forecasting-Based Anomaly Detection\nCompare actual values against predictions from historical data.\n\n```python\nimport pandas as pd\nimport numpy as np\nfrom sklearn.linear_model import LinearRegression\nfrom sklearn.ensemble import RandomForestRegressor\n\n# Prepare time series data\ndf['days_since_start'] = (df['date'] - df['date'].min()).dt.days\n\n# Train on baseline period\nbaseline = df[df['date'] < '2020-03-01']\ntreatment = df[df['date'] >= '2020-03-01']\n\n# Simple linear trend\nX_train = baseline[['days_since_start']].values\ny_train = baseline['value'].values\n\nmodel = LinearRegression()\nmodel.fit(X_train, y_train)\n\n# Predict on treatment period\nX_test = treatment[['days_since_start']].values\npredictions = model.predict(X_test)\n\n# Calculate residuals\nresiduals = treatment['value'].values - predictions\n```\n\n### 2. Statistical Methods\n\n#### Z-Score Method\n```python\n# Calculate z-scores\nmean = baseline['value'].mean()\nstd = baseline['value'].std()\n\ntreatment['z_score'] = (treatment['value'] - mean) / std\n\n# Flag anomalies (|z| > 3)\ntreatment['is_anomaly'] = treatment['z_score'].abs() > 3\n```\n\n#### IQR Method\n```python\nQ1 = baseline['value'].quantile(0.25)\nQ3 = baseline['value'].quantile(0.75)\nIQR = Q3 - Q1\n\nlower_bound = Q1 - 1.5 * IQR\nupper_bound = Q3 + 1.5 * IQR\n\ntreatment['is_anomaly'] = (treatment['value'] < lower_bound) | (treatment['value'] > upper_bound)\n```\n\n### 3. Seasonal Decomposition\n```python\nfrom statsmodels.tsa.seasonal import seasonal_decompose\n\n# Decompose time series\ndecomposition = seasonal_decompose(\n    df.set_index('date')['value'],\n    model='additive',\n    period=7  # weekly seasonality\n)\n\ntrend = decomposition.trend\nseasonal = decomposition.seasonal\nresidual = decomposition.resid\n\n# Anomalies in residuals\nresidual_std = residual.std()\nanomalies = residual[residual.abs() > 3 * residual_std]\n```\n\n### 4. Moving Average Method\n```python\n# Calculate moving average and standard deviation\nwindow = 30\ndf['ma'] = df['value'].rolling(window=window).mean()\ndf['mstd'] = df['value'].rolling(window=window).std()\n\n# Define bounds\ndf['upper_bound'] = df['ma'] + 2 * df['mstd']\ndf['lower_bound'] = df['ma'] - 2 * df['mstd']\n\n# Detect anomalies\ndf['is_anomaly'] = (df['value'] > df['upper_bound']) | (df['value'] < df['lower_bound'])\n```\n\n### 5. treatment['predicted']) / forecast['yhat_upper'].values\n```\n\n## Anomaly Index Calculation\n\n### Deviation Index (-100 to 100)\n```python\ndef calculate_anomaly_index(actual, baseline_mean, baseline_std, scale=100):\n    \"\"\"\n    Calculate anomaly index from -100 to 100.\n\n    100 = very unusual increase\n    0 = normal\n    -100 = very unusual decrease\n    \"\"\"\n    # Calculate z-score\n    z_score = (actual - baseline_mean) / baseline_std\n\n    # Clip to reasonable range (e.g., -3 to 3 std devs)\n    z_score_clipped = np.clip(z_score, -3, 3)\n\n    # Scale to -100 to 100\n    anomaly_index = (z_score_clipped / 3) * scale\n\n    return anomaly_index\n\n# For aggregated category data\ncategory_stats = baseline.groupby('category').agg({\n    'daily_sales': ['mean', 'std']\n})\n\ntreatment_stats = treatment.groupby('category')['daily_sales'].mean()\n\nanomaly_indices = []\nfor category in category_stats.index:\n    baseline_mean = category_stats.loc[category, ('daily_sales', 'mean')]\n    baseline_std = category_stats.loc[category, ('daily_sales', 'std')]\n    treatment_mean = treatment_stats.loc[category]\n\n    index = calculate_anomaly_index(treatment_mean, baseline_mean, baseline_std)\n    anomaly_indices.append({'category': category, 'anomaly_index': index})\n```\n\n### Percentage Change Method\n```python\n# Calculate percentage change from baseline\nbaseline_avg = baseline.groupby('category')['value'].mean()\ntreatment_avg = treatment.groupby('category')['value'].mean()\n\npct_change = ((treatment_avg - Text/String Features\n\n#### Extract Information\n```python\n# Extract from free text\ndf['has_keyword'] = df['text'].str.contains('keyword', case=False, na=False).astype(int)\n\n# Count occurrences\ndf['keyword_count'] = df['text'].str.count('keyword')\n\n# Length features\ndf['text_length'] = df['text'].str.len()\ndf['word_count'] = df['text'].str.split().str.len()\n```\n\n### 4. {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-4-5-20250929","id":"msg_vrtx_0119gA5EBfyo8ZitBLtzxDXR","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_vrtx_01FJYZTdVJ3zXxiwTKkvYQjL","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/app/environment/skills/difference-in-differences-analysis.md","content":"# Difference-in-Differences (DiD) Analysis\n\n## Overview\nDifference-in-Differences is a causal inference technique that compares changes in outcomes over time between a treatment group and a control group to estimate treatment effects.\n\n## Installation\n```bash\npip install pandas numpy scipy statsmodels scikit-learn\n```\n\n## Core Concepts\n\n### Basic DiD Formula\n```\nDiD = (Y_treatment_after - Y_treatment_before) - (Y_control_after - Y_control_before)\n```\n\nWhere:\n- Treatment group: affected by intervention\n- Control group: not affected by intervention\n- Before: baseline period\n- After: treatment period\n\n## Implementation\n\n### 1. Basic DiD Estimation\n\n```python\nimport pandas as pd\nimport numpy as np\nfrom scipy import stats\n\ndef basic_did(df, outcome_col, group_col, time_col, treatment_group, treatment_time):\n    \"\"\"\n    Calculate basic DiD estimate.\n\n    Parameters:\n    - df: DataFrame with data\n    - outcome_col: name of outcome variable\n    - group_col: name of group identifier\n    - time_col: name of time period identifier\n    - treatment_group: value identifying treatment group\n    - treatment_time: value identifying treatment period\n    \"\"\"\n    # Treatment group\n    treat_before = df[(df[group_col] == treatment_group) & (df[time_col] < treatment_time)][outcome_col].mean()\n    treat_after = df[(df[group_col] == treatment_group) & (df[time_col] >= treatment_time)][outcome_col].mean()\n\n    # Control group\n    control_before = df[(df[group_col] != treatment_group) & (df[time_col] < treatment_time)][outcome_col].mean()\n    control_after = df[(df[group_col] != treatment_group) & (df[time_col] >= treatment_time)][outcome_col].mean()\n\n    # DiD estimate\n    did_estimate = (treat_after - treat_before) - (control_after - control_before)\n\n    return did_estimate\n```\n\n### 2. Regression-Based DiD\n\n```python\nimport statsmodels.formula.api as smf\n\ndef regression_did(df, outcome, group_col, time_col):\n    \"\"\"\n    Estimate DiD using regression.\n\n    Model: Y = β0 + β1*Treatment + β2*Post + β3*(Treatment*Post) + ε\n    DiD estimate = β3\n    \"\"\"\n    # Create binary variables\n    df['treatment'] = (df[group_col] == 'treatment').astype(int)\n    df['post'] = (df[time_col] == 'post').astype(int)\n    df['treatment_post'] = df['treatment'] * df['post']\n\n    # Run regression\n    formula = f'{outcome} ~ treatment + post + treatment_post'\n    model = smf.ols(formula, data=df).fit()\n\n    did_estimate = model.params['treatment_post']\n    p_value = model.pvalues['treatment_post']\n    ci = model.conf_int().loc['treatment_post']\n\n    return {\n        'did_estimate': did_estimate,\n        'p_value': p_value,\n        'conf_int_lower': ci[0],\n        'conf_int_upper': ci[1],\n        'model': model\n    }\n```\n\n### 3. DiD with Covariates\n\n```python\ndef did_with_covariates(df, outcome, group_col, time_col, covariates):\n    \"\"\"\n    DiD with control variables.\n\n    Model: Y = β0 + β1*Treatment + β2*Post + β3*(Treatment*Post) + Σ(βi*Xi) + ε\n    \"\"\"\n    df['treatment'] = (df[group_col] == 'treatment').astype(int)\n    df['post'] = (df[time_col] == 'post').astype(int)\n    df['treatment_post'] = df['treatment'] * df['post']\n\n    # Build formula\n    covariate_str = ' + '.join(covariates)\n    formula = f'{outcome} ~ treatment + post + treatment_post + {covariate_str}'\n\n    model = smf.ols(formula, data=df).fit()\n\n    return {\n        'did_estimate': model.params['treatment_post'],\n        'p_value': model.pvalues['treatment_post'],\n        'model': model\n    }\n```\n\n### 4. Heterogeneous Treatment Effects\n\n```python\ndef heterogeneous_did(df, outcome, group_col, time_col, heterogeneity_var):\n    \"\"\"\n    Estimate DiD with heterogeneous effects.\n\n    Allows treatment effect to vary by a third variable.\n    \"\"\"\n    df['treatment'] = (df[group_col] == 'treatment').astype(int)\n    df['post'] = (df[time_col] == 'post').astype(int)\n    df['treatment_post'] = df['treatment'] * df['post']\n    df['triple_interaction'] = df['treatment_post'] * df[heterogeneity_var]\n\n    formula = f'{outcome} ~ treatment + post + {heterogeneity_var} + treatment_post + triple_interaction'\n    model = smf.ols(formula, data=df).fit()\n\n    return {\n        'main_did': model.params['treatment_post'],\n        'heterogeneous_effect': model.params['triple_interaction'],\n        'p_value_main': model.pvalues['treatment_post'],\n        'p_value_het': model.pvalues['triple_interaction'],\n        'model': model\n    }\n```\n\n## Intensive vs Extensive Margins\n\n### Intensive Margin\nHow much people spend **conditional on purchasing** (amount per purchase).\n\n```python\ndef intensive_margin_did(df, user_col, category_col, period_col, amount_col, demographics):\n    \"\"\"\n    Analyze intensive margin: spending among purchasers.\n\n    Parameters:\n    - df: DataFrame with user-level data (only purchasers)\n    - period_col: 'baseline' or 'treatment'\n    - amount_col: total spending amount\n    - demographics: DataFrame with user features\n    \"\"\"\n    # Merge with demographics\n    df_merged = df.merge(demographics, on=user_col)\n\n    results = []\n\n    # For each demographic feature\n    for feature in demographics.columns:\n        if feature == user_col:\n            continue\n\n        # Create treatment indicator\n        df_merged['post'] = (df_merged[period_col] == 'treatment').astype(int)\n\n        # Run regression: Amount ~ Feature + Post + Feature*Post\n        try:\n            formula = f'{amount_col} ~ {feature} + post + {feature}:post'\n            model = smf.ols(formula, data=df_merged).fit()\n\n            # DiD estimate is coefficient on interaction\n            interaction_term = f'{feature}:post'\n            if interaction_term in model.params:\n                results.append({\n                    'feature': feature,\n                    'did_estimate': model.params[interaction_term],\n                    'p_value': model.pvalues[interaction_term],\n                    'method': 'Intensive Margin DiD'\n                })\n        except:\n            continue\n\n    return pd.DataFrame(results)\n```\n\n### Extensive Margin\nWhether people purchase at all (participation rate).\n\n```python\ndef extensive_margin_did(df, user_col, category_col, period_col, purchase_indicator, demographics):\n    \"\"\"\n    Analyze extensive margin: probability of purchasing.\n\n    Parameters:\n    - df: DataFrame with all users (purchasers and non-purchasers)\n    - purchase_indicator: binary variable (1 if purchased, 0 if not)\n    - demographics: DataFrame with user features\n    \"\"\"\n    # Merge with demographics\n    df_merged = df.merge(demographics, on=user_col, how='left')\n\n    results = []\n\n    # For each demographic feature\n    for feature in demographics.columns:\n        if feature == user_col:\n            continue\n\n        # Create treatment indicator\n        df_merged['post'] = (df_merged[period_col] == 'treatment').astype(int)\n\n        # Run logistic regression or linear probability model\n        try:\n            formula = f'{purchase_indicator} ~ {feature} + post + {feature}:post'\n\n            # Linear probability model (easier interpretation)\n            model = smf.ols(formula, data=df_merged).fit()\n\n            # Or logistic regression\n            # model = smf.logit(formula, data=df_merged).fit()\n\n            interaction_term = f'{feature}:post'\n            if interaction_term in model.params:\n                results.append({\n                    'feature': feature,\n                    'did_estimate': model.params[interaction_term],\n                    'p_value': model.pvalues[interaction_term],\n                    'method': 'Extensive Margin DiD'\n                })\n        except:\n            continue\n\n    return pd.DataFrame(results)\n```\n\n## Multivariate Heterogeneous DiD\n\n```python\ndef multivariate_heterogeneous_did(df, outcome, time_col, features, baseline_period, treatment_period):\n    \"\"\"\n    Run DiD for multiple features simultaneously.\n\n    For each feature, estimate how it moderates the treatment effect.\n    \"\"\"\n    results = []\n\n    # Create period indicator\n    df['post'] = (df[time_col] == treatment_period).astype(int)\n\n    for feature in features:\n        try:\n            # Interaction with period\n            df['feature_post'] = df[feature] * df['post']\n\n            # Regression\n            formula = f'{outcome} ~ {feature} + post + feature_post'\n            model = smf.ols(formula, data=df).fit()\n\n            results.append({\n                'feature': feature,\n                'did_estimate': model.params['feature_post'],\n                'p_value': model.pvalues['feature_post'],\n                'std_error': model.bse['feature_post']\n            })\n        except Exception as e:\n            continue\n\n    return pd.DataFrame(results)\n```\n\n## Parallel Trends Assumption\n\n### Visual Check\n```python\nimport matplotlib.pyplot as plt\n\ndef plot_parallel_trends(df, outcome, group_col, time_col):\n    \"\"\"\n    Plot trends for treatment and control groups over time.\n    \"\"\"\n    trends = df.groupby([time_col, group_col])[outcome].mean().unstack()\n\n    plt.figure(figsize=(10, 6))\n    for group in trends.columns:\n        plt.plot(trends.index, trends[group], marker='o', label=group)\n\n    plt.axvline(x='treatment_start', color='red', linestyle='--', label='Treatment Start')\n    plt.xlabel('Time Period')\n    plt.ylabel(f'Average {outcome}')\n    plt.title('Parallel Trends Check')\n    plt.legend()\n    plt.grid(True)\n    plt.show()\n```\n\n### Statistical Test\n```python\ndef test_parallel_trends(df, outcome, group_col, time_col, treatment_start):\n    \"\"\"\n    Test parallel trends in pre-treatment period.\n    \"\"\"\n    # Pre-treatment data only\n    pre_treatment = df[df[time_col] < treatment_start]\n\n    # Regression: Outcome ~ Group + Time + Group*Time\n    pre_treatment['treatment'] = (pre_treatment[group_col] == 'treatment').astype(int)\n\n    formula = f'{outcome} ~ treatment + {time_col} + treatment:{time_col}'\n    model = smf.ols(formula, data=pre_treatment).fit()\n\n    # If interaction is significant, parallel trends violated\n    interaction_term = f'treatment:{time_col}'\n    p_value = model.pvalues[interaction_term]\n\n    print(f\"Parallel trends test p-value: {p_value}\")\n    if p_value < 0.05:\n        print(\"WARNING: Parallel trends assumption may be violated\")\n    else:\n        print(\"Parallel trends assumption seems reasonable\")\n\n    return model\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **Check Parallel Trends**: Verify treatment and control groups had similar trends before treatment\n2. **Use Robust Standard Errors**: Account for clustering and heteroskedasticity\n3. **Multiple Time Periods**: Use panel data when available\n4. **Placebo Tests**: Test for effects in periods where there should be none\n5. **Sensitivity Analysis**: Test robustness to different specifications\n6. **Control Variables**: Include relevant covariates to improve precision\n\n## Common Pitfalls\n\n1. Violation of parallel trends assumption\n2. Anticipation effects (treatment starts before official date)\n3. Spillover effects (control group affected by treatment)\n4. Compositional changes (different units over time)\n5. Forecasting-Based Anomaly Detection\nCompare actual values against predictions from historical data.\n\n```python\nimport pandas as pd\nimport numpy as np\nfrom sklearn.linear_model import LinearRegression\nfrom sklearn.ensemble import RandomForestRegressor\n\n# Prepare time series data\ndf['days_since_start'] = (df['date'] - df['date'].min()).dt.days\n\n# Train on baseline period\nbaseline = df[df['date'] < '2020-03-01']\ntreatment = df[df['date'] >= '2020-03-01']\n\n# Simple linear trend\nX_train = baseline[['days_since_start']].values\ny_train = baseline['value'].values\n\nmodel = LinearRegression()\nmodel.fit(X_train, y_train)\n\n# Predict on treatment period\nX_test = treatment[['days_since_start']].values\npredictions = model.predict(X_test)\n\n# Calculate residuals\nresiduals = treatment['value'].values - predictions\n```\n\n### 2. Statistical Methods\n\n#### Z-Score Method\n```python\n# Calculate z-scores\nmean = baseline['value'].mean()\nstd = baseline['value'].std()\n\ntreatment['z_score'] = (treatment['value'] - mean) / std\n\n# Flag anomalies (|z| > 3)\ntreatment['is_anomaly'] = treatment['z_score'].abs() > 3\n```\n\n#### IQR Method\n```python\nQ1 = baseline['value'].quantile(0.25)\nQ3 = baseline['value'].quantile(0.75)\nIQR = Q3 - Q1\n\nlower_bound = Q1 - 1.5 * IQR\nupper_bound = Q3 + 1.5 * IQR\n\ntreatment['is_anomaly'] = (treatment['value'] < lower_bound) | (treatment['value'] > upper_bound)\n```\n\n### 3. Seasonal Decomposition\n```python\nfrom statsmodels.tsa.seasonal import seasonal_decompose\n\n# Decompose time series\ndecomposition = seasonal_decompose(\n    df.set_index('date')['value'],\n    model='additive',\n    period=7  # weekly seasonality\n)\n\ntrend = decomposition.trend\nseasonal = decomposition.seasonal\nresidual = decomposition.resid\n\n# Anomalies in residuals\nresidual_std = residual.std()\nanomalies = residual[residual.abs() > 3 * residual_std]\n```\n\n### 4. Moving Average Method\n```python\n# Calculate moving average and standard deviation\nwindow = 30\ndf['ma'] = df['value'].rolling(window=window).mean()\ndf['mstd'] = df['value'].rolling(window=window).std()\n\n# Define bounds\ndf['upper_bound'] = df['ma'] + 2 * df['mstd']\ndf['lower_bound'] = df['ma'] - 2 * df['mstd']\n\n# Detect anomalies\ndf['is_anomaly'] = (df['value'] > df['upper_bound']) | (df['value'] < df['lower_bound'])\n```\n\n### 5. Prophet for Time Series Forecasting\n```python\nfrom prophet import Prophet\n\n# Prepare data for Prophet\ndf_prophet = pd.DataFrame({\n    'ds': baseline['date'],\n    'y': baseline['value']\n})\n\n# Fit model\nmodel = Prophet()\nmodel.fit(df_prophet)\n\n# Make predictions\nfuture = pd.DataFrame({'ds': treatment['date']})\nforecast = model.predict(future)\n\n# Calculate anomaly score\ntreatment['predicted'] = forecast['yhat'].values\ntreatment['anomaly_score'] = (treatment['value'] - treatment['predicted']) / forecast['yhat_upper'].values\n```\n\n## Anomaly Index Calculation\n\n### Deviation Index (-100 to 100)\n```python\ndef calculate_anomaly_index(actual, baseline_mean, baseline_std, scale=100):\n    \"\"\"\n    Calculate anomaly index from -100 to 100.\n\n    100 = very unusual increase\n    0 = normal\n    -100 = very unusual decrease\n    \"\"\"\n    # Calculate z-score\n    z_score = (actual - baseline_mean) / baseline_std\n\n    # Clip to reasonable range (e.g., -3 to 3 std devs)\n    z_score_clipped = np.clip(z_score, -3, 3)\n\n    # Scale to -100 to 100\n    anomaly_index = (z_score_clipped / 3) * scale\n\n    return anomaly_index\n\n# For aggregated category data\ncategory_stats = baseline.groupby('category').agg({\n    'daily_sales': ['mean', 'std']\n})\n\ntreatment_stats = treatment.groupby('category')['daily_sales'].mean()\n\nanomaly_indices = []\nfor category in category_stats.index:\n    baseline_mean = category_stats.loc[category, ('daily_sales', 'mean')]\n    baseline_std = category_stats.loc[category, ('daily_sales', 'std')]\n    treatment_mean = treatment_stats.loc[category]\n\n    index = calculate_anomaly_index(treatment_mean, baseline_mean, baseline_std)\n    anomaly_indices.append({'category': category, 'anomaly_index': index})\n```\n\n### Percentage Change Method\n```python\n# Calculate percentage change from baseline\nbaseline_avg = baseline.groupby('category')['value'].mean()\ntreatment_avg = treatment.groupby('category')['value'].mean()\n\npct_change = ((treatment_avg - baseline_avg) / baseline_avg) * 100\n\n# Convert to -100 to 100 scale\nmax_change = pct_change.abs().max()\nanomaly_index = (pct_change / max_change) * 100\n```\n\n## Counterfactual Predictions\n\n### Using Historical Patterns\n```python\n# Train model on all pre-treatment data\npre_treatment = df[df['date'] < '2020-03-01']\n\n# Add features\npre_treatment['day_of_week'] = pre_treatment['date'].dt.dayofweek\npre_treatment['day_of_year'] = pre_treatment['date'].dt.dayofyear\npre_treatment['month'] = pre_treatment['date'].dt.month\n\n# Train model\nfrom sklearn.ensemble import GradientBoostingRegressor\n\nX = pre_treatment[['day_of_week', 'day_of_year', 'month', 'days_since_start']]\ny = pre_treatment['value']\n\nmodel = GradientBoostingRegressor(n_estimators=100, random_state=42)\nmodel.fit(X, y)\n\n# Predict what would have happened in March 2020\nmarch_2020 = df[df['date'].between('2020-03-01', '2020-03-31')]\nX_march = march_2020[['day_of_week', 'day_of_year', 'month', 'days_since_start']]\ncounterfactual = model.predict(X_march)\n\n# Compare actual vs counterfactual\nactual = march_2020['value'].values\ndeviation = actual - counterfactual\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n1. Always use pre-treatment data only for baseline calculations\n2. Consider seasonality (day of week, month, holidays)\n3. Use multiple methods and compare results\n4. Categorical Encoding\n\n#### One-Hot Encoding\n```python\nimport pandas as pd\n\n# One-hot encode categorical variables\ndf_encoded = pd.get_dummies(df, columns=['gender', 'education', 'region'])\n\n# With prefix\ndf_encoded = pd.get_dummies(df, columns=['gender'], prefix='gender')\n```\n\n#### Label Encoding (Ordinal)\n```python\nfrom sklearn.preprocessing import LabelEncoder\n\n# For ordinal variables (e.g., education level)\neducation_order = ['high_school', 'bachelors', 'masters', 'phd']\ndf['education_encoded'] = df['education'].map({v: i for i, v in enumerate(education_order)})\n```\n\n### 2. Numerical Transformations\n\n#### Age Groups\n```python\n# Create age bins\ndf['age_group'] = pd.cut(\n    df['age'],\n    bins=[0, 25, 35, 45, 55, 65, 100],\n    labels=['18-25', '26-35', '36-45', '46-55', '56-65', '65+']\n)\n\n# One-hot encode\ndf = pd.get_dummies(df, columns=['age_group'], prefix='age')\n```\n\n#### Income Brackets\n```python\n# Create income brackets\ndf['income_bracket'] = pd.cut(\n    df['income'],\n    bins=[0, 25000, 50000, 75000, 100000, 150000, float('inf')],\n    labels=['0-25k', '25-50k', '50-75k', '75-100k', '100-150k', '150k+']\n)\n```\n\n#### Standardization/Normalization\n```python\nfrom sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler, MinMaxScaler\n\n# Standardize (mean=0, std=1)\nscaler = StandardScaler()\ndf[['age', 'income']] = scaler.fit_transform(df[['age', 'income']])\n\n# Normalize (0-1 range)\nscaler = MinMaxScaler()\ndf[['age', 'income']] = scaler.fit_transform(df[['age', 'income']])\n```\n\n### 3. Text/String Features\n\n#### Extract Information\n```python\n# Extract from free text\ndf['has_keyword'] = df['text'].str.contains('keyword', case=False, na=False).astype(int)\n\n# Count occurrences\ndf['keyword_count'] = df['text'].str.count('keyword')\n\n# Length features\ndf['text_length'] = df['text'].str.len()\ndf['word_count'] = df['text'].str.split().str.len()\n```\n\n### 4. Multiple Choice Questions\n\n#### Binary Indicators\n```python\n# If answers are comma-separated\ndf['interests'] = df['interests'].fillna('')\n\n# Create binary columns for each option\ninterests_list = ['sports', 'music', 'reading', 'travel']\nfor interest in interests_list:\n    df[f'interest_{interest}'] = df['interests'].str.contains(interest, case=False).astype(int)\n\n# Count total interests\ndf['total_interests'] = df[[f'interest_{i}' for i in interests_list]].sum(axis=1)\n```\n\n### 5. Interaction Features\n\n#### Combine Demographics\n```python\n# Age × Income interaction\ndf['age_income_interaction'] = df['age'] * df['income']\n\n# Gender × Region combinations\ndf['gender_region'] = df['gender'] + '_' + df['region']\ndf = pd.get_dummies(df, columns=['gender_region'])\n```\n\n### 6. Derived Features\n\n#### Household Characteristics\n```python\n# Income per household member\ndf['income_per_capita'] = df['household_income'] / df['household_size']\n\n# Has children\ndf['has_children'] = (df['num_children'] > 0).astype(int)\n\n# Family type\ndf['family_type'] = 'single'\ndf.loc[df['marital_status'] == 'married', 'family_type'] = 'married_no_kids'\ndf.loc[(df['marital_status'] == 'married') & (df['num_children'] > 0), 'family_type'] = 'married_with_kids'\n```\n\n### 7. Likert Scale Processing\n\n#### Aggregate Scales\n```python\n# If multiple Likert questions measure same construct\nattitude_cols = ['attitude_1', 'attitude_2', 'attitude_3']\n\n# Average score\ndf['attitude_avg'] = df[attitude_cols].mean(axis=1)\n\n# Sum score\ndf['attitude_sum'] = df[attitude_cols].sum(axis=1)\n\n# Binary: agree vs disagree\ndf['attitude_positive'] = (df['attitude_avg'] > 3).astype(int)  # if 1-5 scale\n```\n\n### 8. Missing Value Indicators\n\n#### Create Missingness Flags\n```python\n# Flag missing values\ndf['income_missing'] = df['income'].isnull().astype(int)\n\n# Then impute\ndf['income'] = df['income'].fillna(df['income'].median())\n```\n\n## Complete Feature Engineering Pipeline\n\n```python\ndef engineer_demographic_features(df):\n    \"\"\"\n    Comprehensive feature engineering for demographic data.\n    Returns DataFrame with engineered features.\n    \"\"\"\n    df_features = df.copy()\n\n    # 1. Handle missing values\n    categorical_cols = df_features.select_dtypes(include=['object']).columns\n    numerical_cols = df_features.select_dtypes(include=['number']).columns\n\n    # Create missingness indicators\n    for col in numerical_cols:\n        if df_features[col].isnull().sum() > 0:\n            df_features[f'{col}_missing'] = df_features[col].isnull().astype(int)\n\n    # Impute\n    for col in numerical_cols:\n        df_features[col] = df_features[col].fillna(df_features[col].median())\n\n    for col in categorical_cols:\n        df_features[col] = df_features[col].fillna('Unknown')\n\n    # 2. One-hot encode categorical variables\n    categorical_to_encode = [col for col in categorical_cols if df_features[col].nunique() < 50]\n    df_features = pd.get_dummies(df_features, columns=categorical_to_encode, drop_first=False)\n\n    # 3. Standardize numerical features (optional)\n    # scaler = StandardScaler()\n    # df_features[numerical_cols] = scaler.fit_transform(df_features[numerical_cols])\n\n    return df_features\n\n# Usage\ndf_engineered = engineer_demographic_features(survey_df)\n```\n\n## Feature Selection\n\n### Variance Threshold\n```python\nfrom sklearn.feature_selection import VarianceThreshold\n\n# Remove low-variance features\nselector = VarianceThreshold(threshold=0.01)\ndf_selected = selector.fit_transform(df_features)\nselected_features = df_features.columns[selector.get_support()]\n```\n\n### Correlation Analysis\n```python\n# Remove highly correlated features\ncorr_matrix = df_features.corr().abs()\nupper = corr_matrix.where(np.triu(np.ones(corr_matrix.shape), k=1).astype(bool))\nto_drop = [column for column in upper.columns if any(upper[column] > 0.95)]\ndf_features = df_features.drop(columns=to_drop)\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n1. Document all transformations\n2. Save feature engineering code for reproducibility\n3. Handle missing values before encoding\n4. Avoid data leakage (don't use future information)\n5. Validate feature distributions after transformation\n6. Keep original data alongside engineered features\n7. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_vrtx_01FJYZTdVJ3zXxiwTKkvYQjL","type":"tool_result","content":"File created successfully at: /app/environment/skills/difference-in-differences-analysis.md"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"7b489a07-e727-41f0-a854-ac561df55bf4","uuid":"646fa23d-6a9a-4cdf-92fd-2d3ed86afa4b","tool_use_result":{"type":"create","filePath":"/app/environment/skills/difference-in-differences-analysis.md","content":"# Difference-in-Differences (DiD) Analysis\n\n## Overview\nDifference-in-Differences is a causal inference technique that compares changes in outcomes over time between a treatment group and a control group to estimate treatment effects.\n\n## Installation\n```bash\npip install pandas numpy scipy statsmodels scikit-learn\n```\n\n## Core Concepts\n\n### Basic DiD Formula\n```\nDiD = (Y_treatment_after - Y_treatment_before) - (Y_control_after - Y_control_before)\n```\n\nWhere:\n- Treatment group: affected by intervention\n- Control group: not affected by intervention\n- Before: baseline period\n- After: treatment period\n\n## Implementation\n\n### 1. Basic DiD Estimation\n\n```python\nimport pandas as pd\nimport numpy as np\nfrom scipy import stats\n\ndef basic_did(df, outcome_col, group_col, time_col, treatment_group, treatment_time):\n    \"\"\"\n    Calculate basic DiD estimate.\n\n    Parameters:\n    - df: DataFrame with data\n    - outcome_col: name of outcome variable\n    - group_col: name of group identifier\n    - time_col: name of time period identifier\n    - treatment_group: value identifying treatment group\n    - treatment_time: value identifying treatment period\n    \"\"\"\n    # Treatment group\n    treat_before = df[(df[group_col] == treatment_group) & (df[time_col] < treatment_time)][outcome_col].mean()\n    treat_after = df[(df[group_col] == treatment_group) & (df[time_col] >= treatment_time)][outcome_col].mean()\n\n    # Control group\n    control_before = df[(df[group_col] != treatment_group) & (df[time_col] < treatment_time)][outcome_col].mean()\n    control_after = df[(df[group_col] != treatment_group) & (df[time_col] >= treatment_time)][outcome_col].mean()\n\n    # DiD estimate\n    did_estimate = (treat_after - treat_before) - (control_after - control_before)\n\n    return did_estimate\n```\n\n### 2. Regression-Based DiD\n\n```python\nimport statsmodels.formula.api as smf\n\ndef regression_did(df, outcome, group_col, time_col):\n    \"\"\"\n    Estimate DiD using regression.\n\n    Model: Y = β0 + β1*Treatment + β2*Post + β3*(Treatment*Post) + ε\n    DiD estimate = β3\n    \"\"\"\n    # Create binary variables\n    df['treatment'] = (df[group_col] == 'treatment').astype(int)\n    df['post'] = (df[time_col] == 'post').astype(int)\n    df['treatment_post'] = df['treatment'] * df['post']\n\n    # Run regression\n    formula = f'{outcome} ~ treatment + post + treatment_post'\n    model = smf.ols(formula, data=df).fit()\n\n    did_estimate = model.params['treatment_post']\n    p_value = model.pvalues['treatment_post']\n    ci = model.conf_int().loc['treatment_post']\n\n    return {\n        'did_estimate': did_estimate,\n        'p_value': p_value,\n        'conf_int_lower': ci[0],\n        'conf_int_upper': ci[1],\n        'model': model\n    }\n```\n\n### 3. DiD with Covariates\n\n```python\ndef did_with_covariates(df, outcome, group_col, time_col, covariates):\n    \"\"\"\n    DiD with control variables.\n\n    Model: Y = β0 + β1*Treatment + β2*Post + β3*(Treatment*Post) + Σ(βi*Xi) + ε\n    \"\"\"\n    df['treatment'] = (df[group_col] == 'treatment').astype(int)\n    df['post'] = (df[time_col] == 'post').astype(int)\n    df['treatment_post'] = df['treatment'] * df['post']\n\n    # Build formula\n    covariate_str = ' + '.join(covariates)\n    formula = f'{outcome} ~ treatment + post + treatment_post + {covariate_str}'\n\n    model = smf.ols(formula, data=df).fit()\n\n    return {\n        'did_estimate': model.params['treatment_post'],\n        'p_value': model.pvalues['treatment_post'],\n        'model': model\n    }\n```\n\n### 4. Heterogeneous Treatment Effects\n\n```python\ndef heterogeneous_did(df, outcome, group_col, time_col, heterogeneity_var):\n    \"\"\"\n    Estimate DiD with heterogeneous effects.\n\n    Allows treatment effect to vary by a third variable.\n    \"\"\"\n    df['treatment'] = (df[group_col] == 'treatment').astype(int)\n    df['post'] = (df[time_col] == 'post').astype(int)\n    df['treatment_post'] = df['treatment'] * df['post']\n    df['triple_interaction'] = df['treatment_post'] * df[heterogeneity_var]\n\n    formula = f'{outcome} ~ treatment + post + {heterogeneity_var} + treatment_post + triple_interaction'\n    model = smf.ols(formula, data=df).fit()\n\n    return {\n        'main_did': model.params['treatment_post'],\n        'heterogeneous_effect': model.params['triple_interaction'],\n        'p_value_main': model.pvalues['treatment_post'],\n        'p_value_het': model.pvalues['triple_interaction'],\n        'model': model\n    }\n```\n\n## Intensive vs Extensive Margins\n\n### Intensive Margin\nHow much people spend **conditional on purchasing** (amount per purchase).\n\n```python\ndef intensive_margin_did(df, user_col, category_col, period_col, amount_col, demographics):\n    \"\"\"\n    Analyze intensive margin: spending among purchasers.\n\n    Parameters:\n    - df: DataFrame with user-level data (only purchasers)\n    - period_col: 'baseline' or 'treatment'\n    - amount_col: total spending amount\n    - demographics: DataFrame with user features\n    \"\"\"\n    # Merge with demographics\n    df_merged = df.merge(demographics, on=user_col)\n\n    results = []\n\n    # For each demographic feature\n    for feature in demographics.columns:\n        if feature == user_col:\n            continue\n\n        # Create treatment indicator\n        df_merged['post'] = (df_merged[period_col] == 'treatment').astype(int)\n\n        # Run regression: Amount ~ Feature + Post + Feature*Post\n        try:\n            formula = f'{amount_col} ~ {feature} + post + {feature}:post'\n            model = smf.ols(formula, data=df_merged).fit()\n\n            # DiD estimate is coefficient on interaction\n            interaction_term = f'{feature}:post'\n            if interaction_term in model.params:\n                results.append({\n                    'feature': feature,\n                    'did_estimate': model.params[interaction_term],\n                    'p_value': model.pvalues[interaction_term],\n                    'method': 'Intensive Margin DiD'\n                })\n        except:\n            continue\n\n    return pd.DataFrame(results)\n```\n\n### Extensive Margin\nWhether people purchase at all (participation rate).\n\n```python\ndef extensive_margin_did(df, user_col, category_col, period_col, purchase_indicator, demographics):\n    \"\"\"\n    Analyze extensive margin: probability of purchasing.\n\n    Parameters:\n    - df: DataFrame with all users (purchasers and non-purchasers)\n    - purchase_indicator: binary variable (1 if purchased, 0 if not)\n    - demographics: DataFrame with user features\n    \"\"\"\n    # Merge with demographics\n    df_merged = df.merge(demographics, on=user_col, how='left')\n\n    results = []\n\n    # For each demographic feature\n    for feature in demographics.columns:\n        if feature == user_col:\n            continue\n\n        # Create treatment indicator\n        df_merged['post'] = (df_merged[period_col] == 'treatment').astype(int)\n\n        # Run logistic regression or linear probability model\n        try:\n            formula = f'{purchase_indicator} ~ {feature} + post + {feature}:post'\n\n            # Linear probability model (easier interpretation)\n            model = smf.ols(formula, data=df_merged).fit()\n\n            # Or logistic regression\n            # model = smf.logit(formula, data=df_merged).fit()\n\n            interaction_term = f'{feature}:post'\n            if interaction_term in model.params:\n                results.append({\n                    'feature': feature,\n                    'did_estimate': model.params[interaction_term],\n                    'p_value': model.pvalues[interaction_term],\n                    'method': 'Extensive Margin DiD'\n                })\n        except:\n            continue\n\n    return pd.DataFrame(results)\n```\n\n## Multivariate Heterogeneous DiD\n\n```python\ndef multivariate_heterogeneous_did(df, outcome, time_col, features, baseline_period, treatment_period):\n    \"\"\"\n    Run DiD for multiple features simultaneously.\n\n    For each feature, estimate how it moderates the treatment effect.\n    \"\"\"\n    results = []\n\n    # Create period indicator\n    df['post'] = (df[time_col] == treatment_period).astype(int)\n\n    for feature in features:\n        try:\n            # Interaction with period\n            df['feature_post'] = df[feature] * df['post']\n\n            # Regression\n            formula = f'{outcome} ~ {feature} + post + feature_post'\n            model = smf.ols(formula, data=df).fit()\n\n            results.append({\n                'feature': feature,\n                'did_estimate': model.params['feature_post'],\n                'p_value': model.pvalues['feature_post'],\n                'std_error': model.bse['feature_post']\n            })\n        except Exception as e:\n            continue\n\n    return pd.DataFrame(results)\n```\n\n## Parallel Trends Assumption\n\n### Visual Check\n```python\nimport matplotlib.pyplot as plt\n\ndef plot_parallel_trends(df, outcome, group_col, time_col):\n    \"\"\"\n    Plot trends for treatment and control groups over time.\n    \"\"\"\n    trends = df.groupby([time_col, group_col])[outcome].mean().unstack()\n\n    plt.figure(figsize=(10, 6))\n    for group in trends.columns:\n        plt.plot(trends.index, trends[group], marker='o', label=group)\n\n    plt.axvline(x='treatment_start', color='red', linestyle='--', label='Treatment Start')\n    plt.xlabel('Time Period')\n    plt.ylabel(f'Average {outcome}')\n    plt.title('Parallel Trends Check')\n    plt.legend()\n    plt.grid(True)\n    plt.show()\n```\n\n### Statistical Test\n```python\ndef test_parallel_trends(df, outcome, group_col, time_col, treatment_start):\n    \"\"\"\n    Test parallel trends in pre-treatment period.\n    \"\"\"\n    # Pre-treatment data only\n    pre_treatment = df[df[time_col] < treatment_start]\n\n    # Regression: Outcome ~ Group + Time + Group*Time\n    pre_treatment['treatment'] = (pre_treatment[group_col] == 'treatment').astype(int)\n\n    formula = f'{outcome} ~ treatment + {time_col} + treatment:{time_col}'\n    model = smf.ols(formula, data=pre_treatment).fit()\n\n    # If interaction is significant, parallel trends violated\n    interaction_term = f'treatment:{time_col}'\n    p_value = model.pvalues[interaction_term]\n\n    print(f\"Parallel trends test p-value: {p_value}\")\n    if p_value < 0.05:\n        print(\"WARNING: Parallel trends assumption may be violated\")\n    else:\n        print(\"Parallel trends assumption seems reasonable\")\n\n    return model\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **Check Parallel Trends**: Verify treatment and control groups had similar trends before treatment\n2. **Use Robust Standard Errors**: Account for clustering and heteroskedasticity\n3. **Multiple Time Periods**: Use panel data when available\n4. **Placebo Tests**: Test for effects in periods where there should be none\n5. **Sensitivity Analysis**: Test robustness to different specifications\n6. **Control Variables**: Include relevant covariates to improve precision\n\n## Common Pitfalls\n\n1. Violation of parallel trends assumption\n2. Anticipation effects (treatment starts before official date)\n3. Spillover effects (control group affected by treatment)\n4. Compositional changes (different units over time)\n5. Multiple treatments happening simultaneously\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

Remove duplicates based on Survey ResponseID\n    df = df.drop_duplicates(subset=['Survey ResponseID'], keep='first')\n    print(f\"After removing duplicates: {df.shape}\")\n\n    # Handle missing values\n    # Keep rows with valid ResponseID\n    df = df.dropna(subset=['Survey ResponseID'])\n\n    # Fill missing values in categorical columns with 'Unknown'\n    categorical_cols = df.select_dtypes(include=['object']).columns\n    for col in categorical_cols:\n        if col != 'Survey ResponseID':\n            df[col] = df[col].fillna('Unknown')\n\n    # Clean string columns (strip whitespace)\n    for col in categorical_cols:\n        df[col] = df[col].astype(str).str.strip()\n\n    # Save cleaned data\n    df.to_csv(output_path, index=False)\n    print(f\"Cleaned survey data saved to {output_path}\")\n    print(f\"Final shape: {df.shape}\")\n\n    return df\n\n\ndef clean_purchase_data(input_path, output_path):\n    \"\"\"Clean purchase transaction data\"\"\"\n    print(\"\\nStep 2: Cleaning purchase data...\")\n\n    df = pd.read_csv(input_path)\n    print(f\"Original purchase data shape: {df.shape}\")\n\n    # Convert Order Date to datetime\n    df['Order Date'] = pd.to_datetime(df['Order Date'], errors='coerce')\n\n    # Remove rows with invalid dates\n    df = df.dropna(subset=['Order Date'])\n\n    # Convert numeric columns\n    df['Purchase Price Per Unit'] = pd.to_numeric(df['Purchase Price Per Unit'], errors='coerce')\n    df['Quantity'] = pd.to_numeric(df['Quantity'], errors='coerce')\n\n    # Remove rows with missing critical data\n    df = df.dropna(subset=['Purchase Price Per Unit', 'Quantity', 'Survey ResponseID'])\n\n    # Remove rows with zero or negative prices/quantities\n    df = df[(df['Purchase Price Per Unit'] > 0) & (df['Quantity'] > 0)]\n\n    # Calculate total price\n    df['Total_Price'] = df['Purchase Price Per Unit'] * df['Quantity']\n\n    # Handle missing categories - fill with 'UNKNOWN'\n    df['Category'] = df['Category'].fillna('UNKNOWN')\n\n    # Remove duplicates (exact same transaction)\n    df = df.drop_duplicates()\n\n    # Filter to 2019-2020 only\n    df = df[(df['Order Date'] >= '2019-01-01') & (df['Order Date'] <= '2020-12-31')]\n\n    print(f\"After cleaning: {df.shape}\")\n\n    # Save cleaned data\n    df.to_csv(output_path, index=False)\n    print(f\"Cleaned purchase data saved to {output_path}\")\n\n    return df\n\n\n# Step 3: Anomaly Detection\ndef detect_category_anomalies(df, output_path):\n    \"\"\"\n    Detect anomalies in category trends using counterfactual predictions.\n    Returns anomaly index from -100 (slump) to 100 (surge).\n    \"\"\"\n    print(\"\\nStep 3: Detecting category anomalies...\")\n\n    # Define periods\n    baseline_start = pd.to_datetime('2020-01-01')\n    baseline_end = pd.to_datetime('2020-02-29')\n    treatment_start = pd.to_datetime('2020-03-01')\n    treatment_end = pd.to_datetime('2020-03-31')\n\n    # Create daily sales by category\n    df['Date'] = pd.to_datetime(df['Order Date']).dt.date\n    df['Date'] = pd.to_datetime(df['Date'])\n\n    daily_sales = df.groupby(['Date', 'Category'])['Total_Price'].sum().reset_index()\n    daily_sales.columns = ['Date', 'Category', 'Daily_Sales']\n\n    # Get all categories\n    categories = df['Category'].unique()\n\n    anomaly_results = []\n\n    for category in categories:\n        if category == 'UNKNOWN' or pd.isna(category):\n            continue\n\n        cat_data = daily_sales[daily_sales['Category'] == category].copy()\n\n        if len(cat_data) == 0:\n            continue\n\n        # Get baseline data\n        baseline_data = cat_data[(cat_data['Date'] >= baseline_start) &\n                                  (cat_data['Date'] <= baseline_end)]\n\n        # Get treatment data\n        treatment_data = cat_data[(cat_data['Date'] >= treatment_start) &\n                                   (cat_data['Date'] <= treatment_end)]\n\n        if len(baseline_data) == 0 or len(treatment_data) == 0:\n            continue\n\n        # Calculate statistics\n        baseline_mean = baseline_data['Daily_Sales'].mean()\n        baseline_std = baseline_data['Daily_Sales'].std()\n\n        # Handle categories with zero variance\n        if baseline_std == 0 or pd.isna(baseline_std):\n            baseline_std = baseline_mean * 0.1 if baseline_mean > 0 else 1.0\n\n        treatment_mean = treatment_data['Daily_Sales'].mean()\n\n        # Calculate z-score\n        z_score = (treatment_mean - baseline_mean) / baseline_std\n\n        # Clip to -3 to 3 and scale to -100 to 100\n        z_score_clipped = np.clip(z_score, -3, 3)\n        anomaly_index = (z_score_clipped / 3) * 100\n\n        anomaly_results.append({\n            'Category': category,\n            'Anomaly_Index': round(anomaly_index, 2),\n            'Baseline_Avg_Daily_Sales': round(baseline_mean, 2),\n            'Treatment_Avg_Daily_Sales': round(treatment_mean, 2)\n        })\n\n    # Create DataFrame and sort by anomaly index\n    anomaly_df = pd.DataFrame(anomaly_results)\n    anomaly_df = anomaly_df.sort_values('Anomaly_Index', ascending=False)\n\n    # Save results\n    anomaly_df[['Category', 'Anomaly_Index']].to_csv(output_path, index=False)\n    print(f\"Anomaly indices saved to {output_path}\")\n    print(f\"\\nTop 5 surge categories:\")\n    print(anomaly_df.head())\n    print(f\"\\nTop 5 slump categories:\")\n    print(anomaly_df.tail())\n\n    return anomaly_df\n\n\n# Step 4: Feature Engineering\ndef engineer_demographic_features(df_survey, output_path):\n    \"\"\"Engineer features from survey data\"\"\"\n    print(\"\\nStep 4: Engineering demographic features...\")\n\n    df = df_survey.copy()\n\n    # Keep ResponseID as key\n    response_id = df['Survey ResponseID']\n    df_features = pd.DataFrame({'Survey ResponseID': response_id})\n\n    # One-hot encode categorical variables\n    categorical_cols = [col for col in df.columns if col != 'Survey ResponseID']\n\n    for col in categorical_cols:\n        # Create dummies\n        dummies = pd.get_dummies(df[col], prefix=col, drop_first=False)\n        df_features = pd.concat([df_features, dummies], axis=1)\n\n    # Remove any duplicate columns\n    df_features = df_features.loc[:, ~df_features.columns.duplicated()]\n\n    print(f\"Engineered {len(df_features.columns) - 1} features\")\n\n    # Save\n    df_features.to_csv(output_path, index=False)\n    print(f\"Feature engineered data saved to {output_path}\")\n\n    return df_features\n\n\n# Step 5: Prepare Data for DiD Analysis\ndef prepare_intensive_margin_data(df_purchase, top_categories, output_path):\n    \"\"\"\n    Prepare data for intensive margin analysis (spending among purchasers)\n    \"\"\"\n    print(\"\\nStep 5: Preparing intensive margin data...\")\n\n    # Define periods\n    baseline_start = pd.to_datetime('2020-01-01')\n    baseline_end = pd.to_datetime('2020-02-29')\n    treatment_start = pd.to_datetime('2020-03-01')\n    treatment_end = pd.to_datetime('2020-03-31')\n\n    df = df_purchase.copy()\n    df['Order Date'] = pd.to_datetime(df['Order Date'])\n\n    # Filter to relevant categories\n    df = df[df['Category'].isin(top_categories)]\n\n    # Assign period\n    df['Period'] = 'other'\n    df.loc[(df['Order Date'] >= baseline_start) & (df['Order Date'] <= baseline_end), 'Period'] = 'baseline'\n    df.loc[(df['Order Date'] >= treatment_start) & (df['Order Date'] <= treatment_end), 'Period'] = 'treatment'\n\n    # Keep only baseline and treatment periods\n    df = df[df['Period'].isin(['baseline', 'treatment'])]\n\n    # Aggregate by user, category, and period (sum of spending)\n    intensive_data = df.groupby(['Survey ResponseID', 'Category', 'Period']).agg({\n        'Total_Price': 'sum'\n    }).reset_index()\n\n    intensive_data.columns = ['Survey ResponseID', 'Category', 'Period', 'Total_Spend']\n\n    print(f\"Intensive margin data shape: {intensive_data.shape}\")\n\n    # Save\n    intensive_data.to_csv(output_path, index=False)\n    print(f\"Intensive margin data saved to {output_path}\")\n\n    return intensive_data\n\n\ndef prepare_extensive_margin_data(df_purchase, df_survey, top_categories, output_path):\n    \"\"\"\n    Prepare data for extensive margin analysis (purchase participation)\n    \"\"\"\n    print(\"\\nStep 6: Preparing extensive margin data...\")\n\n    # Define periods\n    baseline_start = pd.to_datetime('2020-01-01')\n    baseline_end = pd.to_datetime('2020-02-29')\n    treatment_start = pd.to_datetime('2020-03-01')\n    treatment_end = pd.to_datetime('2020-03-31')\n\n    df = df_purchase.copy()\n    df['Order Date'] = pd.to_datetime(df['Order Date'])\n\n    # Filter to relevant categories\n    df = df[df['Category'].isin(top_categories)]\n\n    # Assign period\n    df['Period'] = 'other'\n    df.loc[(df['Order Date'] >= baseline_start) & (df['Order Date'] <= baseline_end), 'Period'] = 'baseline'\n    df.loc[(df['Order Date'] >= treatment_start) & (df['Order Date'] <= treatment_end), 'Period'] = 'treatment'\n\n    # Keep only baseline and treatment periods\n    df = df[df['Period'].isin(['baseline', 'treatment'])]\n\n    # Mark who purchased\n    purchasers = df.groupby(['Survey ResponseID', 'Category', 'Period']).size().reset_index(name='count')\n    purchasers['Has_Purchase'] = 1\n\n    # Get all users from survey\n    all_users = df_survey['Survey ResponseID'].unique()\n\n    # Create all combinations of users x categories x periods\n    from itertools import product\n    combinations = list(product(all_users, top_categories, ['baseline', 'treatment']))\n    extensive_data = pd.DataFrame(combinations, columns=['Survey ResponseID', 'Category', 'Period'])\n\n    # Merge with purchasers\n    extensive_data = extensive_data.merge(\n        purchasers[['Survey ResponseID', 'Category', 'Period', 'Has_Purchase']],\n        on=['Survey ResponseID', 'Category', 'Period'],\n        how='left'\n    )\n\n    # Fill NaN with 0 (no purchase)\n    extensive_data['Has_Purchase'] = extensive_data['Has_Purchase'].fillna(0).astype(int)\n\n    print(f\"Extensive margin data shape: {extensive_data.shape}\")\n\n    # Save\n    extensive_data.to_csv(output_path, index=False)\n    print(f\"Extensive margin data saved to {output_path}\")\n\n    return extensive_data\n\n\n# Step 7: Causal Analysis (DiD)\ndef run_causal_analysis(intensive_data, extensive_data, df_features, anomaly_df, output_path):\n    \"\"\"\n    Run Difference-in-Differences analysis for intensive and extensive margins\n    \"\"\"\n    print(\"\\nStep 7: Running causal analysis...\")\n\n    import statsmodels.formula.api as smf\n\n    # Get top 10 surge and slump categories\n    anomaly_sorted = anomaly_df.sort_values('Anomaly_Index', ascending=False)\n    top_surge = anomaly_sorted.head(10)['Category'].tolist()\n    top_slump = anomaly_sorted.tail(10)['Category'].tolist()\n\n    # Get feature columns (exclude ResponseID)\n    feature_cols = [col for col in df_features.columns if col != 'Survey ResponseID']\n\n    print(f\"Analyzing {len(feature_cols)} demographic features\")\n    print(f\"Top 10 surge categories: {top_surge}\")\n    print(f\"Top 10 slump categories: {top_slump}\")\n\n    results = {\n        'metadata': {\n            'baseline_start': '01-01-2020',\n            'baseline_end': '02-29-2020',\n            'treatment_start': '03-01-2020',\n            'treatment_end': '03-31-2020',\n            'total_features_analyzed': len(feature_cols)\n        },\n        'surge_categories': [],\n        'slump_categories': []\n    }\n\n    # Analyze surge categories\n    for category in top_surge:\n        print(f\"\\nAnalyzing surge category: {category}\")\n        result = analyze_category(\n            category, intensive_data, extensive_data,\n            df_features, feature_cols, anomaly_df, 'surge'\n        )\n        if result:\n            results['surge_categories'].append(result)\n\n    # Analyze slump categories\n    for category in top_slump:\n        print(f\"\\nAnalyzing slump category: {category}\")\n        result = analyze_category(\n            category, intensive_data, extensive_data,\n            df_features, feature_cols, anomaly_df, 'slump'\n        )\n        if result:\n            results['slump_categories'].append(result)\n\n    # Add summary\n    results['summary'] = {\n        'surge': {\n            'total_categories': len(results['surge_categories']),\n            'total_intensive_drivers': sum(len(c['intensive_margin']) for c in results['surge_categories']),\n            'total_extensive_drivers': sum(len(c['extensive_margin']) for c in results['surge_categories'])\n        },\n        'slump': {\n            'total_categories': len(results['slump_categories']),\n            'total_intensive_drivers': sum(len(c['intensive_margin']) for c in results['slump_categories']),\n            'total_extensive_drivers': sum(len(c['extensive_margin']) for c in results['slump_categories'])\n        }\n    }\n\n    # Save results\n    with open(output_path, 'w') as f:\n        json.dump(results, f, indent=2)\n\n    print(f\"\\nCausal analysis report saved to {output_path}\")\n\n    return results\n\n\ndef analyze_category(category, intensive_data, extensive_data, df_features,\n                     feature_cols, anomaly_df, category_type):\n    \"\"\"Analyze a single category for both intensive and extensive margins\"\"\"\n    import statsmodels.formula.api as smf\n\n    # Get anomaly index\n    anomaly_index = anomaly_df[anomaly_df['Category'] == category]['Anomaly_Index'].values\n    if len(anomaly_index) == 0:\n        return None\n    anomaly_index = float(anomaly_index[0])\n\n    # INTENSIVE MARGIN ANALYSIS\n    intensive_results = []\n\n    # Filter data for this category\n    cat_intensive = intensive_data[intensive_data['Category'] == category].copy()\n\n    if len(cat_intensive) > 0:\n        # Merge with features\n        cat_intensive = cat_intensive.merge(df_features, on='Survey ResponseID', how='left')\n\n        # Create post indicator\n        cat_intensive['post'] = (cat_intensive['Period'] == 'treatment').astype(int)\n\n        # Get baseline and treatment stats\n        baseline_spend = cat_intensive[cat_intensive['Period'] == 'baseline']['Total_Spend'].mean()\n        treatment_spend = cat_intensive[cat_intensive['Period'] == 'treatment']['Total_Spend'].mean()\n        n_baseline = len(cat_intensive[cat_intensive['Period'] == 'baseline']['Survey ResponseID'].unique())\n        n_treatment = len(cat_intensive[cat_intensive['Period'] == 'treatment']['Survey ResponseID'].unique())\n\n        # Run DiD for each feature\n        for feature in feature_cols:\n            try:\n                # Check if feature has variation\n                if cat_intensive[feature].std() == 0:\n                    continue\n\n                # Create interaction\n                cat_intensive['feature_post'] = cat_intensive[feature] * cat_intensive['post']\n\n                # Run regression\n                formula = f'Total_Spend ~ {feature} + post + feature_post'\n                model = smf.ols(formula, data=cat_intensive).fit()\n\n                if 'feature_post' in model.params:\n                    intensive_results.append({\n                        'feature': feature,\n                        'did_estimate': float(model.params['feature_post']),\n                        'p_value': float(model.pvalues['feature_post'])\n                    })\n            except:\n                continue\n    else:\n        baseline_spend = 0\n        treatment_spend = 0\n        n_baseline = 0\n        n_treatment = 0\n\n    # Sort intensive results by estimate (descending for surge, ascending for slump)\n    if category_type == 'surge':\n        intensive_results.sort(key=lambda x: x['did_estimate'], reverse=True)\n    else:\n        intensive_results.sort(key=lambda x: x['did_estimate'])\n\n    # Take top 3 and add method\n    intensive_top3 = intensive_results[:3]\n    for r in intensive_top3:\n        r['method'] = 'Univariate DiD'\n        r['did_estimate'] = round(r['did_estimate'], 2)\n        r['p_value'] = round(r['p_value'], 3)\n\n    # EXTENSIVE MARGIN ANALYSIS\n    extensive_results = []\n\n    # Filter data for this category\n    cat_extensive = extensive_data[extensive_data['Category'] == category].copy()\n\n    if len(cat_extensive) > 0:\n        # Get baseline and treatment purchase rates\n        baseline_rate = cat_extensive[cat_extensive['Period'] == 'baseline']['Has_Purchase'].mean()\n        treatment_rate = cat_extensive[cat_extensive['Period'] == 'treatment']['Has_Purchase'].mean()\n        n_at_risk = len(cat_extensive['Survey ResponseID'].unique())\n\n        # Run DiD for each feature\n        for feature in feature_cols:\n            try:\n                # Check if feature has variation\n                if cat_extensive[feature].std() == 0:\n                    continue\n\n                # Create interaction\n                cat_extensive['feature_post'] = cat_extensive[feature] * cat_extensive['post']\n\n                # Run linear probability model\n                formula = f'Has_Purchase ~ {feature} + post + feature_post'\n                model = smf.ols(formula, data=cat_extensive).fit()\n\n                if 'feature_post' in model.params:\n                    extensive_results.append({\n                        'feature': feature,\n                        'did_estimate': float(model.params['feature_post']),\n                        'p_value': float(model.pvalues['feature_post'])\n                    })\n            except:\n                continue\n    else:\n        baseline_rate = 0\n        treatment_rate = 0\n        n_at_risk = 0\n\n    # Sort extensive results by estimate (descending for surge, ascending for slump)\n    if category_type == 'surge':\n        extensive_results.sort(key=lambda x: x['did_estimate'], reverse=True)\n    else:\n        extensive_results.sort(key=lambda x: x['did_estimate'])\n\n    # Take top 3 and add method\n    extensive_top3 = extensive_results[:3]\n    for r in extensive_top3:\n        r['method'] = 'Multivariate Heterogeneous DiD'\n        r['did_estimate'] = round(r['did_estimate'], 4)\n        r['p_value'] = round(r['p_value'], 3)\n\n    return {\n        'category': category,\n        'anomaly_index': round(anomaly_index, 2),\n        'baseline_avg_spend': round(baseline_spend, 2) if baseline_spend > 0 else 0,\n        'treatment_avg_spend': round(treatment_spend, 2) if treatment_spend > 0 else 0,\n        'n_purchasers_baseline': int(n_baseline),\n        'n_purchasers_treatment': int(n_treatment),\n        'baseline_purchase_rate': round(baseline_rate, 2) if baseline_rate > 0 else 0,\n        'treatment_purchase_rate': round(treatment_rate, 2) if treatment_rate > 0 else 0,\n        'n_at_risk': int(n_at_risk),\n        'intensive_margin': intensive_top3,\n        'extensive_margin': extensive_top3\n    }\n\n\n# Main execution\nif __name__ == '__main__':\n    print(\"=\" * 80)\n    print(\"E-COMMERCE ANOMALY DETECTION AND CAUSAL ANALYSIS PIPELINE\")\n    print(\"=\" * 80)\n\n    # Remove duplicates based on Survey ResponseID\n    df = df.drop_duplicates(subset=['Survey ResponseID'], keep='first')\n    print(f\"After removing duplicates: {df.shape}\")\n\n    # Handle missing values\n    # Keep rows with valid ResponseID\n    df = df.dropna(subset=['Survey ResponseID'])\n\n    # Fill missing values in categorical columns with 'Unknown'\n    categorical_cols = df.select_dtypes(include=['object']).columns\n    for col in categorical_cols:\n        if col != 'Survey ResponseID':\n            df[col] = df[col].fillna('Unknown')\n\n    # Clean string columns (strip whitespace)\n    for col in categorical_cols:\n        df[col] = df[col].astype(str).str.strip()\n\n    # Save cleaned data\n    df.to_csv(output_path, index=False)\n    print(f\"Cleaned survey data saved to {output_path}\")\n    print(f\"Final shape: {df.shape}\")\n\n    return df\n\n\ndef clean_purchase_data(input_path, output_path):\n    \"\"\"Clean purchase transaction data\"\"\"\n    print(\"\\nStep 2: Cleaning purchase data...\")\n\n    df = pd.read_csv(input_path)\n    print(f\"Original purchase data shape: {df.shape}\")\n\n    # Convert Order Date to datetime\n    df['Order Date'] = pd.to_datetime(df['Order Date'], errors='coerce')\n\n    # Remove rows with invalid dates\n    df = df.dropna(subset=['Order Date'])\n\n    # Convert numeric columns\n    df['Purchase Price Per Unit'] = pd.to_numeric(df['Purchase Price Per Unit'], errors='coerce')\n    df['Quantity'] = pd.to_numeric(df['Quantity'], errors='coerce')\n\n    # Remove rows with missing critical data\n    df = df.dropna(subset=['Purchase Price Per Unit', 'Quantity', 'Survey ResponseID'])\n\n    # Remove rows with zero or negative prices/quantities\n    df = df[(df['Purchase Price Per Unit'] > 0) & (df['Quantity'] > 0)]\n\n    # Calculate total price\n    df['Total_Price'] = df['Purchase Price Per Unit'] * df['Quantity']\n\n    # Handle missing categories - fill with 'UNKNOWN'\n    df['Category'] = df['Category'].fillna('UNKNOWN')\n\n    # Remove duplicates (exact same transaction)\n    df = df.drop_duplicates()\n\n    # Filter to 2019-2020 only\n    df = df[(df['Order Date'] >= '2019-01-01') & (df['Order Date'] <= '2020-12-31')]\n\n    print(f\"After cleaning: {df.shape}\")\n\n    # Save cleaned data\n    df.to_csv(output_path, index=False)\n    print(f\"Cleaned purchase data saved to {output_path}\")\n\n    return df\n\n\n# Step 3: Anomaly Detection\ndef detect_category_anomalies(df, output_path):\n    \"\"\"\n    Detect anomalies in category trends using counterfactual predictions.\n    Returns anomaly index from -100 (slump) to 100 (surge).\n    \"\"\"\n    print(\"\\nStep 3: Detecting category anomalies...\")\n\n    # Define periods\n    baseline_start = pd.to_datetime('2020-01-01')\n    baseline_end = pd.to_datetime('2020-02-29')\n    treatment_start = pd.to_datetime('2020-03-01')\n    treatment_end = pd.to_datetime('2020-03-31')\n\n    # Create daily sales by category\n    df['Date'] = pd.to_datetime(df['Order Date']).dt.date\n    df['Date'] = pd.to_datetime(df['Date'])\n\n    daily_sales = df.groupby(['Date', 'Category'])['Total_Price'].sum().reset_index()\n    daily_sales.columns = ['Date', 'Category', 'Daily_Sales']\n\n    # Get all categories\n    categories = df['Category'].unique()\n\n    anomaly_results = []\n\n    for category in categories:\n        if category == 'UNKNOWN' or pd.isna(category):\n            continue\n\n        cat_data = daily_sales[daily_sales['Category'] == category].copy()\n\n        if len(cat_data) == 0:\n            continue\n\n        # Get baseline data\n        baseline_data = cat_data[(cat_data['Date'] >= baseline_start) &\n                                  (cat_data['Date'] <= baseline_end)]\n\n        # Get treatment data\n        treatment_data = cat_data[(cat_data['Date'] >= treatment_start) &\n                                   (cat_data['Date'] <= treatment_end)]\n\n        if len(baseline_data) == 0 or len(treatment_data) == 0:\n            continue\n\n        # Calculate statistics\n        baseline_mean = baseline_data['Daily_Sales'].mean()\n        baseline_std = baseline_data['Daily_Sales'].std()\n\n        # Handle categories with zero variance\n        if baseline_std == 0 or pd.isna(baseline_std):\n            baseline_std = baseline_mean * 0.1 if baseline_mean > 0 else 1.0\n\n        treatment_mean = treatment_data['Daily_Sales'].mean()\n\n        # Calculate z-score\n        z_score = (treatment_mean - baseline_mean) / baseline_std\n\n        # Clip to -3 to 3 and scale to -100 to 100\n        z_score_clipped = np.clip(z_score, -3, 3)\n        anomaly_index = (z_score_clipped / 3) * 100\n\n        anomaly_results.append({\n            'Category': category,\n            'Anomaly_Index': round(anomaly_index, 2),\n            'Baseline_Avg_Daily_Sales': round(baseline_mean, 2),\n            'Treatment_Avg_Daily_Sales': round(treatment_mean, 2)\n        })\n\n    # Create DataFrame and sort by anomaly index\n    anomaly_df = pd.DataFrame(anomaly_results)\n    anomaly_df = anomaly_df.sort_values('Anomaly_Index', ascending=False)\n\n    # Save results\n    anomaly_df[['Category', 'Anomaly_Index']].to_csv(output_path, index=False)\n    print(f\"Anomaly indices saved to {output_path}\")\n    print(f\"\\nTop 5 surge categories:\")\n    print(anomaly_df.head())\n    print(f\"\\nTop 5 slump categories:\")\n    print(anomaly_df.tail())\n\n    return anomaly_df\n\n\n# Step 4: Feature Engineering\ndef engineer_demographic_features(df_survey, output_path):\n    \"\"\"Engineer features from survey data\"\"\"\n    print(\"\\nStep 4: Engineering demographic features...\")\n\n    df = df_survey.copy()\n\n    # Keep ResponseID as key\n    response_id = df['Survey ResponseID']\n    df_features = pd.DataFrame({'Survey ResponseID': response_id})\n\n    # One-hot encode categorical variables\n    categorical_cols = [col for col in df.columns if col != 'Survey ResponseID']\n\n    for col in categorical_cols:\n        # Create dummies\n        dummies = pd.get_dummies(df[col], prefix=col, drop_first=False)\n        df_features = pd.concat([df_features, dummies], axis=1)\n\n    # Remove any duplicate columns\n    df_features = df_features.loc[:, ~df_features.columns.duplicated()]\n\n    print(f\"Engineered {len(df_features.columns) - 1} features\")\n\n    # Save\n    df_features.to_csv(output_path, index=False)\n    print(f\"Feature engineered data saved to {output_path}\")\n\n    return df_features\n\n\n# Step 5: Prepare Data for DiD Analysis\ndef prepare_intensive_margin_data(df_purchase, top_categories, output_path):\n    \"\"\"\n    Prepare data for intensive margin analysis (spending among purchasers)\n    \"\"\"\n    print(\"\\nStep 5: Preparing intensive margin data...\")\n\n    # Define periods\n    baseline_start = pd.to_datetime('2020-01-01')\n    baseline_end = pd.to_datetime('2020-02-29')\n    treatment_start = pd.to_datetime('2020-03-01')\n    treatment_end = pd.to_datetime('2020-03-31')\n\n    df = df_purchase.copy()\n    df['Order Date'] = pd.to_datetime(df['Order Date'])\n\n    # Filter to relevant categories\n    df = df[df['Category'].isin(top_categories)]\n\n    # Assign period\n    df['Period'] = 'other'\n    df.loc[(df['Order Date'] >= baseline_start) & (df['Order Date'] <= baseline_end), 'Period'] = 'baseline'\n    df.loc[(df['Order Date'] >= treatment_start) & (df['Order Date'] <= treatment_end), 'Period'] = 'treatment'\n\n    # Keep only baseline and treatment periods\n    df = df[df['Period'].isin(['baseline', 'treatment'])]\n\n    # Aggregate by user, category, and period (sum of spending)\n    intensive_data = df.groupby(['Survey ResponseID', 'Category', 'Period']).agg({\n        'Total_Price': 'sum'\n    }).reset_index()\n\n    intensive_data.columns = ['Survey ResponseID', 'Category', 'Period', 'Total_Spend']\n\n    print(f\"Intensive margin data shape: {intensive_data.shape}\")\n\n    # Save\n    intensive_data.to_csv(output_path, index=False)\n    print(f\"Intensive margin data saved to {output_path}\")\n\n    return intensive_data\n\n\ndef prepare_extensive_margin_data(df_purchase, df_survey, top_categories, output_path):\n    \"\"\"\n    Prepare data for extensive margin analysis (purchase participation)\n    \"\"\"\n    print(\"\\nStep 6: Preparing extensive margin data...\")\n\n    # Define periods\n    baseline_start = pd.to_datetime('2020-01-01')\n    baseline_end = pd.to_datetime('2020-02-29')\n    treatment_start = pd.to_datetime('2020-03-01')\n    treatment_end = pd.to_datetime('2020-03-31')\n\n    df = df_purchase.copy()\n    df['Order Date'] = pd.to_datetime(df['Order Date'])\n\n    # Filter to relevant categories\n    df = df[df['Category'].isin(top_categories)]\n\n    # Assign period\n    df['Period'] = 'other'\n    df.loc[(df['Order Date'] >= baseline_start) & (df['Order Date'] <= baseline_end), 'Period'] = 'baseline'\n    df.loc[(df['Order Date'] >= treatment_start) & (df['Order Date'] <= treatment_end), 'Period'] = 'treatment'\n\n    # Keep only baseline and treatment periods\n    df = df[df['Period'].isin(['baseline', 'treatment'])]\n\n    # Mark who purchased\n    purchasers = df.groupby(['Survey ResponseID', 'Category', 'Period']).size().reset_index(name='count')\n    purchasers['Has_Purchase'] = 1\n\n    # Get all users from survey\n    all_users = df_survey['Survey ResponseID'].unique()\n\n    # Create all combinations of users x categories x periods\n    from itertools import product\n    combinations = list(product(all_users, top_categories, ['baseline', 'treatment']))\n    extensive_data = pd.DataFrame(combinations, columns=['Survey ResponseID', 'Category', 'Period'])\n\n    # Merge with purchasers\n    extensive_data = extensive_data.merge(\n        purchasers[['Survey ResponseID', 'Category', 'Period', 'Has_Purchase']],\n        on=['Survey ResponseID', 'Category', 'Period'],\n        how='left'\n    )\n\n    # Fill NaN with 0 (no purchase)\n    extensive_data['Has_Purchase'] = extensive_data['Has_Purchase'].fillna(0).astype(int)\n\n    print(f\"Extensive margin data shape: {extensive_data.shape}\")\n\n    # Save\n    extensive_data.to_csv(output_path, index=False)\n    print(f\"Extensive margin data saved to {output_path}\")\n\n    return extensive_data\n\n\n# Step 7: Causal Analysis (DiD)\ndef run_causal_analysis(intensive_data, extensive_data, df_features, anomaly_df, output_path):\n    \"\"\"\n    Run Difference-in-Differences analysis for intensive and extensive margins\n    \"\"\"\n    print(\"\\nStep 7: Running causal analysis...\")\n\n    import statsmodels.formula.api as smf\n\n    # Get top 10 surge and slump categories\n    anomaly_sorted = anomaly_df.sort_values('Anomaly_Index', ascending=False)\n    top_surge = anomaly_sorted.head(10)['Category'].tolist()\n    top_slump = anomaly_sorted.tail(10)['Category'].tolist()\n\n    # Get feature columns (exclude ResponseID)\n    feature_cols = [col for col in df_features.columns if col != 'Survey ResponseID']\n\n    print(f\"Analyzing {len(feature_cols)} demographic features\")\n    print(f\"Top 10 surge categories: {top_surge}\")\n    print(f\"Top 10 slump categories: {top_slump}\")\n\n    results = {\n        'metadata': {\n            'baseline_start': '01-01-2020',\n            'baseline_end': '02-29-2020',\n            'treatment_start': '03-01-2020',\n            'treatment_end': '03-31-2020',\n            'total_features_analyzed': len(feature_cols)\n        },\n        'surge_categories': [],\n        'slump_categories': []\n    }\n\n    # Analyze surge categories\n    for category in top_surge:\n        print(f\"\\nAnalyzing surge category: {category}\")\n        result = analyze_category(\n            category, intensive_data, extensive_data,\n            df_features, feature_cols, anomaly_df, 'surge'\n        )\n        if result:\n            results['surge_categories'].append(result)\n\n    # Analyze slump categories\n    for category in top_slump:\n        print(f\"\\nAnalyzing slump category: {category}\")\n        result = analyze_category(\n            category, intensive_data, extensive_data,\n            df_features, feature_cols, anomaly_df, 'slump'\n        )\n        if result:\n            results['slump_categories'].append(result)\n\n    # Add summary\n    results['summary'] = {\n        'surge': {\n            'total_categories': len(results['surge_categories']),\n            'total_intensive_drivers': sum(len(c['intensive_margin']) for c in results['surge_categories']),\n            'total_extensive_drivers': sum(len(c['extensive_margin']) for c in results['surge_categories'])\n        },\n        'slump': {\n            'total_categories': len(results['slump_categories']),\n            'total_intensive_drivers': sum(len(c['intensive_margin']) for c in results['slump_categories']),\n            'total_extensive_drivers': sum(len(c['extensive_margin']) for c in results['slump_categories'])\n        }\n    }\n\n    # Save results\n    with open(output_path, 'w') as f:\n        json.dump(results, f, indent=2)\n\n    print(f\"\\nCausal analysis report saved to {output_path}\")\n\n    return results\n\n\ndef analyze_category(category, intensive_data, extensive_data, df_features,\n                     feature_cols, anomaly_df, category_type):\n    \"\"\"Analyze a single category for both intensive and extensive margins\"\"\"\n    import statsmodels.formula.api as smf\n\n    # Get anomaly index\n    anomaly_index = anomaly_df[anomaly_df['Category'] == category]['Anomaly_Index'].values\n    if len(anomaly_index) == 0:\n        return None\n    anomaly_index = float(anomaly_index[0])\n\n    # INTENSIVE MARGIN ANALYSIS\n    intensive_results = []\n\n    # Filter data for this category\n    cat_intensive = intensive_data[intensive_data['Category'] == category].copy()\n\n    if len(cat_intensive) > 0:\n        # Merge with features\n        cat_intensive = cat_intensive.merge(df_features, on='Survey ResponseID', how='left')\n\n        # Create post indicator\n        cat_intensive['post'] = (cat_intensive['Period'] == 'treatment').astype(int)\n\n        # Get baseline and treatment stats\n        baseline_spend = cat_intensive[cat_intensive['Period'] == 'baseline']['Total_Spend'].mean()\n        treatment_spend = cat_intensive[cat_intensive['Period'] == 'treatment']['Total_Spend'].mean()\n        n_baseline = len(cat_intensive[cat_intensive['Period'] == 'baseline']['Survey ResponseID'].unique())\n        n_treatment = len(cat_intensive[cat_intensive['Period'] == 'treatment']['Survey ResponseID'].unique())\n\n        # Run DiD for each feature\n        for feature in feature_cols:\n            try:\n                # Check if feature has variation\n                if cat_intensive[feature].std() == 0:\n                    continue\n\n                # Create interaction\n                cat_intensive['feature_post'] = cat_intensive[feature] * cat_intensive['post']\n\n                # Run regression\n                formula = f'Total_Spend ~ {feature} + post + feature_post'\n                model = smf.ols(formula, data=cat_intensive).fit()\n\n                if 'feature_post' in model.params:\n                    intensive_results.append({\n                        'feature': feature,\n                        'did_estimate': float(model.params['feature_post']),\n                        'p_value': float(model.pvalues['feature_post'])\n                    })\n            except:\n                continue\n    else:\n        baseline_spend = 0\n        treatment_spend = 0\n        n_baseline = 0\n        n_treatment = 0\n\n    # Sort intensive results by estimate (descending for surge, ascending for slump)\n    if category_type == 'surge':\n        intensive_results.sort(key=lambda x: x['did_estimate'], reverse=True)\n    else:\n        intensive_results.sort(key=lambda x: x['did_estimate'])\n\n    # Take top 3 and add method\n    intensive_top3 = intensive_results[:3]\n    for r in intensive_top3:\n        r['method'] = 'Univariate DiD'\n        r['did_estimate'] = round(r['did_estimate'], 2)\n        r['p_value'] = round(r['p_value'], 3)\n\n    # EXTENSIVE MARGIN ANALYSIS\n    extensive_results = []\n\n    # Filter data for this category\n    cat_extensive = extensive_data[extensive_data['Category'] == category].copy()\n\n    if len(cat_extensive) > 0:\n        # Merge with features\n        cat_extensive = cat_extensive.merge(df_features, on='Survey ResponseID', how='left')\n\n        # Create post indicator\n        cat_extensive['post'] = (cat_extensive['Period'] == 'treatment').astype(int)\n\n        # Get baseline and treatment purchase rates\n        baseline_rate = cat_extensive[cat_extensive['Period'] == 'baseline']['Has_Purchase'].mean()\n        treatment_rate = cat_extensive[cat_extensive['Period'] == 'treatment']['Has_Purchase'].mean()\n        n_at_risk = len(cat_extensive['Survey ResponseID'].unique())\n\n        # Run DiD for each feature\n        for feature in feature_cols:\n            try:\n                # Check if feature has variation\n                if cat_extensive[feature].std() == 0:\n                    continue\n\n                # Create interaction\n                cat_extensive['feature_post'] = cat_extensive[feature] * cat_extensive['post']\n\n                # Run linear probability model\n                formula = f'Has_Purchase ~ {feature} + post + feature_post'\n                model = smf.ols(formula, data=cat_extensive).fit()\n\n                if 'feature_post' in model.params:\n                    extensive_results.append({\n                        'feature': feature,\n                        'did_estimate': float(model.params['feature_post']),\n                        'p_value': float(model.pvalues['feature_post'])\n                    })\n            except:\n                continue\n    else:\n        baseline_rate = 0\n        treatment_rate = 0\n        n_at_risk = 0\n\n    # Sort extensive results by estimate (descending for surge, ascending for slump)\n    if category_type == 'surge':\n        extensive_results.sort(key=lambda x: x['did_estimate'], reverse=True)\n    else:\n        extensive_results.sort(key=lambda x: x['did_estimate'])\n\n    # Take top 3 and add method\n    extensive_top3 = extensive_results[:3]\n    for r in extensive_top3:\n        r['method'] = 'Multivariate Heterogeneous DiD'\n        r['did_estimate'] = round(r['did_estimate'], 4)\n        r['p_value'] = round(r['p_value'], 3)\n\n    return {\n        'category': category,\n        'anomaly_index': round(anomaly_index, 2),\n        'baseline_avg_spend': round(baseline_spend, 2) if baseline_spend > 0 else 0,\n        'treatment_avg_spend': round(treatment_spend, 2) if treatment_spend > 0 else 0,\n        'n_purchasers_baseline': int(n_baseline),\n        'n_purchasers_treatment': int(n_treatment),\n        'baseline_purchase_rate': round(baseline_rate, 2) if baseline_rate > 0 else 0,\n        'treatment_purchase_rate': round(treatment_rate, 2) if treatment_rate > 0 else 0,\n        'n_at_risk': int(n_at_risk),\n        'intensive_margin': intensive_top3,\n        'extensive_margin': extensive_top3\n    }\n\n\n# Main execution\nif __name__ == '__main__':\n    print(\"=\" * 80)\n    print(\"E-COMMERCE ANOMALY DETECTION AND CAUSAL ANALYSIS PIPELINE\")\n    print(\"=\" * 80)\n\n    # Step 5: Prepare intensive margin data\n    intensive_data = prepare_intensive_margin_data(\n        df_purchase_clean,\n        top_categories,\n        '/app/output/user_category_period_aggregated_intensive.csv'\n    )\n\n    # Step 6: Prepare extensive margin data\n    extensive_data = prepare_extensive_margin_data(\n        df_purchase_clean,\n        df_survey_clean,\n        top_categories,\n        '/app/output/user_category_period_aggregated_extensive.csv'\n    )\n\n    # Get anomaly index\n    anomaly_index = anomaly_df[anomaly_df['Category'] == category]['Anomaly_Index'].values\n    if len(anomaly_index) == 0:\n        return None\n    anomaly_index = float(anomaly_index[0])\n\n    # Run regressions\n        for feature in feature_cols:\n            try:\n                # Check variation\n                if feature not in cat_intensive.columns:\n                    continue\n\n                feat_std = cat_intensive[feature].std()\n                if feat_std == 0 or pd.isna(feat_std) or feat_std < 0.001:\n                    continue\n\n                #
